# PLAN — the single source of truth (projects · tasks · ETAs)

**This file is THE plan.** Every task lives here, under a project, with an `ETA` date and a `status`.
The long-horizon plan (the 6/22 launch) lives here too — as dated tasks, not a separate doc. There is no
second plan surface: the calendar is phone reminders only, the harness #-task list is a session scratch
mirror, `CHANGELOG.md`/git hold history. If it's planned, it's a line here.

**Line format** (strict — parsed by `scripts/check_plan.py`):
`- #<id> | <YYYY-MM-DD ETA> | <status> | <title>`   ·  status ∈ pending · in_progress · blocked

**The two rituals operate ONLY on this file:**
- **OPEN** (every session): run `python scripts/check_plan.py --today` → it prints OVERDUE + due-today open
  tasks. That list IS the day's plan. State it before reacting to anything.
- **CLOSE** (every session): every open task's ETA must be ≥ today. Rebump any past-due one to a real
  future date (or close it). File every new item as a line here under a project. Then commit — the
  pre-commit gate (`check_plan.py`) REFUSES the commit if any open task lacks a project, an ETA, or has a
  past ETA, or if an open harness task is missing from this file. No gaps, mechanically.

_Last CLOSE: 2026-06-16._

---

## Live-money cutover — MAGNA53 (the GO-direction gates)

> 📜 **THE PRINCIPLES (P1-P10) + THE GOAL: `docs/roadmap/ep_profitability_program.md` § THE PRINCIPLES.** The operator's own rules, in precedence order — P1 recall first (a real EP must never be missed) · P2 art over rigid thresholds · P3 tail not average · P4 return on capital · P5 selection is broader · P6 bias for action · P7 reassess after every path · P8 conclusions are provisional. **Read before any analysis, card or proposal on EP work; cite by name.** ⚖ THE LINE sits above all of them.

- #570 | 2026-08-26 | pending | **The SILENT D-1 UNIVERSE FLOORS — they drop the fattest tails in the dataset, and they are never logged.** Found 2026-08-16 in the missed-winner attribution: 12 of the 63 true detection misses were killed by prior-day volume <50k shares or prior close <$5 — **real rules that write no skip_reason anywhere**, so they were invisible until a market-wide scan found them. 🔴 They carry the FATTEST tails of any miss class: **median 21.9×ADR against 11.3× for the whole missed set.** Mechanism: **the floors read the PRIOR day's volume and price, while the opportunity is created ON the gap day** — a name illiquid on D-1 that becomes tier-A tradeable on D0 is excluded by a measurement taken before the event that makes it tradeable. ▶ **DoD: (a) the floors LOG a skip_reason like every other gate, so this class stops being invisible; (b) a read of what a same-day liquidity check would have admitted, in ADR units.** ⚠ Changing the floors themselves is entry discipline = THE LINE; this task makes them visible and measures them, and any change is his fork. [The Real EP Plan — Selection surface] >> 🔴 **SCOPE-ADD, OPERATOR-DIRECTED 2026-08-17: the 10% GAP FLOOR is the same disease and it fired on a live name today.** His words: *"an arbitrary 10% threshold, especially when it's actually near it, is not the best filter that determines what we trade."* **THE CASE — ARGX 2026-08-17:** alerted at 10.5%, the 9:31 real-time check read 9.5%, skipped `setup:gap_below_floor`. By 10:15 ET it was **+14.8%, sitting at 91% of its range, having cleared its prior all-time high (953.58) by 3% and holding**, moving 5.1x a normal day, with **zero extension** (the prior close WAS the lowest close of the last 5 sessions). That is the top-quartile profile from the weekend ranking work — small gap, un-extended, tight base, ladder fully cleared — and a one-instant reading 0.5 points under a round number is what stopped it. 🔴 **THE DEEPER DEFECT, measured: the input disagrees with itself, systematically and in ONE direction.** All 7 gap-floor skips on record (2026-08-06 to 08-14) show the alert gap far ABOVE the 9:31 real-time gap — U 18.4→1.0 · LFST 18.8→−2.0 · ACMR 15.1→6.6 · TH 12.2→−5.0 · SE 10.6→9.2 · GLBE 10.7→4.8 · VERA 14.2→5.6. **7 of 7 in the same direction is a basis mismatch or a systematic premarket-fade, not noise** — we are gating on a number that does not agree with the one that raised the alert. Related: `ep_rt_prev_close_mismatch` fires on real vendor disagreement (BYND alpaca 0.407 vs polygon 12.21 on 08-14). **Fix the MEASUREMENT before arguing about the THRESHOLD.** 📊 **OUTCOMES of those 7 (raw %, N far short of the bar — NOT evidence to change anything):** 5 of 7 rose; U reached +28.8% over 20d and +27.2% at 5d; only ACMR (−13.1% at 5d, 0.0 MFE) was a clean save. ⚠ Raw percent is the WRONG unit (the weekend's central lesson) and these are not realized-R under our mechanics — redo in ADR units and under the live bracket before anyone cites them. ▶ **ARM ADDED TO THIS TASK'S DoD:** (c) the gap floor gets the same treatment as the D-1 floors — measure what it has skipped, in ADR units and realized-R, and separate genuine fades from measurement disagreement; (d) evaluate whether a gap magnitude belongs as a RANKING INPUT rather than a hard gate, and whether a near-miss band should exist at all. ⚖ **THE LINE UNCHANGED — this task MEASURES. Any change to the floor is his fork, needs CHANGE_PROCESS + N>=10 + sign-off.** ⚠ ARGX is the 8th case and the first with a full intraday record; capture its outcome. >> 📌 **ARGX CLOSED 2026-08-17 — the case is now complete and it went the operator's way.** Open 927.33 (+8.93%, the reading that skipped it) · High **1002.16** · Close **987.84 = +16.04% on the day**, finishing at 81% of its range. **Cleared the prior all-time high of 953.58 by 5.09% and held it into the close.** 5.5x a normal day's range. Volume 683,963 = **2.4x its 20-day average**. Extension at entry was ZERO — the prior close WAS the lowest close of the last 5 sessions. So on the day we skipped it: small gap, no extension, tight base, all-time-high clear, 2.4x volume, closed near the high. We still have that partially off so we're filtering alerts due to using delayed data."* He is right, and it is already measured.** **⚠ RETRACTED — THIS SENTENCE IS WRONG, see the correction later on this line: `EP_RT_PASS2_ENABLED` is `true` on prod and `_pass1_gap_floor()` resolves to 5.0. ~~`EP_RT_PASS2_ENABLED` defaults to `false` and is UNSET on prod → Pass 2 is OFF.~~** So `_pass1_gap_floor()` applies the REAL 10% floor to the **~15-minute-DELAYED** universe screen instead of the 5% superset. Funnel over 246 settled ticker-days: 216 never alerted → **only 49 were still ≥10% at the ACTUAL OPEN** (137 faded then crossed intraday, 30 never saw 10% again) → **32 pass the mechanical gates** (market cap killed 17, dollar-volume 7). Of those 32, **ZERO reached ≥8×ADR**, P90 4.2×, median 1.2× — against the alerted population's 2 of 100 (P90 3.7×) and the alerted crossers' 2 of 28. **The would-have-alerted set is no better than what we already alert on.** 44 of the 49 were seen by the delayed scan later the same day and rejected ON MERIT; the pure never-seen class is **5 names in three weeks**. **Measured prize: 0 additional tail winners per month against a target of ~1.** ⚠ **100% of BOTH cohorts are right-censored** (median 8 of 20 forward sessions) — every share is a FLOOR, and PLPC 6.2× / PGY 5.4× / COHU 4.8× are still running. **Re-read after 2026-09-15.** ⚠ Today's 6 catches were excluded as unsettled, which excludes the most extreme case on record: **WEAV, real-time 32.91% vs delayed 0.36%, opened +31.8% and holding at 6× a normal day.** 🔑 **WHERE THE WINNERS ACTUALLY LIVE, and it points straight back at THIS task's gap-floor arm: the FADED-THEN-CROSSED class (137 names) holds the real ones — BCAR opened −2.5% and ran 14.7×ADR.** That is unreachable by ANY feed change under the current entry re-check; it is the floor-TIMING fork (the ARGX case), not a data-latency problem. ⚠ Untested: catalyst/RVOL/rank gates were not replayed, so 32 is an UPPER bound and the true loss is smaller. >> ✅ **THE FADED-THEN-CROSSED CLASS IS ANSWERED 2026-08-18 — and it is NOT a floor-timing loss** (`docs/analysis/490b_faded_crossed_timing_2026-08-18.md`, $0, read-only, thresholds pre-registered). **It is an OPENING-RANGE animal, not an afternoon one:** of 121 timed crossers, **88% cross 10% inside 09:30-09:45** (median open gap +4.3%; only 10 of 137 opened DOWN), 11 cross 09:45-10:00, **zero between 10:00 and noon**, 3 after noon. **The rule DILUTES rather than concentrates:** crossed-by-09:45 = 104 names / 2 winners (1.9%); after replaying our own mechanical gates = 64 names / 1 winner (1.6%) — against the alerted book on the SAME dates, recomputed fresh, at 100 alerts / 2 winners (2.0%). Waiving the cooldown too would give +1.3/month at ~84 extra candidates/month — i.e. at the book's own hit rate, not a better one. ⚠ Censoring: EROC 07-30 sits at 7.89×ADR with 8 sessions left — but ITS blocker was the top-20 rank cap, still not the floor. **Clean re-read after 2026-09-15.** ▶ **THIS ARM CAN REST. He experiences it as *"every day"* because a different caller trips each night. ▶ **THE FIX SHAPE (not yet built):** stop treating `max_tokens` as a text budget. Either set `thinking` EXPLICITLY per caller — `ThinkingConfigDisabledParam` for schema-bounded callers like theme_validation (`{"remove": [...]}`), where thinking adds nothing and can consume everything — or recover on `stop_reason=max_tokens` by retrying once with thinking off. ⚠ `budget_tokens` is REJECTED on sonnet-5, so a thinking budget is NOT available; on/off is the only lever. ⚠ **A significance test would NOT have caught the sibling news-drift false alarm and a bigger ceiling will not catch this** — both were confounded, not noisy. ⚠ **NOTE the prod SDK is now anthropic 1.0.0** (pinned 08-20) and its `messages.create` DOES accept `thinking` — verified in-container. ⏩ **PULLED FORWARD 2026-09-02 → 2026-08-22** (earlier, so NOT a rebump): the operator approved the aggressive weekend plan and this is a Saturday card. If theme quality visibly degrades, this is the first thing to look at.

subscription. **Run result: 28 already covered · 767 fetched · 86 thin (genuinely illiquid names, ZERO fetch errors) · 853 API calls · 7.4 minutes · $0.** ⚠ **A REAL BUG IT CAUGHT MID-FLIGHT, worth carrying:** its first coverage query did `$date::date + interval` inside SQL, which Postgres resolved in the DB's UTC session timezone rather than ET — silently checking the wrong 6.5-hour window and making every already-covered day look uncovered. **That is the ET/UTC bug class CLAUDE.md warns about, one layer down in SQL rather than Python.** Fixed by binding tz-aware Python datetimes directly (the `order_manager.py` pattern) and re-verified by running twice. 🚩 **WEEKEND PRIORITY #1, set 2026-08-21 — this is now ahead of #577.** The reassessment: we have TWO theories of why EP does not make money, and only one has direct evidence. **(A) ADMISSION** — today's gate ranking says extension + top-20 exclude **55 names that later doubled**. **(B) CONVERSION** — the rehearsal (§0g) already showed **15 surfaced tail winners → 3 traded → ALL lost**, and the replay of 5 fills netted **−2.0R**. 🔑 **B is the damning one: we already SURFACE winners and fail to convert them.** Loosening admission pours more names into a funnel converting at ~0 — which is the operator's own **P9** (tight downstream selectivity is what BUYS loose upstream admission) pointed at ourselves. 🔬 **And the rehearsal already named the mechanism this task tests:** the move starts **7-21 sessions AFTER the EP day**, and in 3 of 5 cases the launching base formed days later and **BELOW the EP-day low** — so the ORB-day entry is simply the wrong moment for most of them. ✅ **The data only just became testable** (backfill: 532k bars / 893 following-session ticker-days), and answering it needs **NO admission change and no operator fork** — it is pure measurement on names we already surface. 🔴 **ANSWERED 2026-08-22 — HONEST NEGATIVE, and it MOVES the problem. `docs/analysis/delayed_entry_562_2026-08-22.md`.** All 8 spec triggers priced on 44 stop-out episodes / 346,601 minute bars, pre-registered before outcomes. **None clears the bar.** Best arm = 620 turn within 0.5×ADR of the EP-day close: fires 31/44, 39% win, +0.21R/trade — but median trade **−1.00R** and ex-top-2 **−3.74R**. 🔑 **THE DECISIVE NUMBER: eight arms, ZERO banked-positive.** Every arm's CLOSED trades net negative (−3.7R to −19.0R); the study's entire positive R sits in two STILL-OPEN August marks (SMCI +5.45R, TEAM +4.77R, both `data_end`). The discipline that matters — unrealised marks were NOT allowed to carry the result. 🎯 **THE MECHANISM, and it reframes the whole conversion thesis: the triggers FIND the turns — the STOPS die first.** The arms re-entered all three known tail winners (INTC / SMCI / NRIX), and roughly half the 620-arm stop-outs sat in front of a **≥2×ADR run that began after the stop hit**. Median stop distance was **1.5-2.2%** (NET's was **0.27%**) — noise-width. Doubling the stop flips nothing. **This is the same disease §0g measured at the ORB entry, now reproduced at the DELAYED entry.** ⚖ **THE FORK, and it is the operator's: entry LOCATION is not the binding constraint — STOP WIDTH RELATIVE TO ADR is.** That is exit discipline = THE LINE. Stated, not proposed. ▶ **THE ONE UNTESTED ARM, and it is HIS OWN MOVE: day-0 re-entry.** The TEAM case (stopped 09:43, back in 12:05) was excluded by a pre-registered following-sessions-only window. **The day-0 bars EXIST and it is testable at $0** — run it next. ▶ Also: the August cluster is unsettled; a free re-run ~mid-Sept decides weak-negative vs clean-negative. 🔴 **NOT CONCLUDED — operator 2026-08-22: *"Delayed entry needs to be worked on more, let's not conclude anything yet."* Re-opened as OPEN-ENDED research, not a closed negative.** 🎯 **HIS FRAMING, verbatim and it is the spec:** *"Goal is it help us get back into real EPs IF we were shaken out on day 1 ORB entry… we can scale the # of retries depending on how confident we think it's a real EP, for example, if super confident of real-EP we may even have multiple retries and if less confident we have 1 retry… the goal as stated is to get us into and stay in real EPs."* ⚠ **WHY THE NEGATIVE DOES NOT STAND: the study used the WRONG POPULATION.** All 44 episodes were stop-outs, NOT confirmed real EPs. Re-entry is a technique for staying in a REAL EP — testing it on names that were never real EPs measures the wrong thing. **Re-run on the 26 labelled real EPs (`tests/fixtures/must_not_miss_eps.py`), not on all stop-outs.** This is the selectivity coupling he has raised repeatedly (P9). 🟢 **AND THE MECHANISM IS ALREADY LIVE — I was wrong to call same-day re-entry unpriced.** `order_manager.attempt_day1_reentry` exists and fires on a day-1 stop-out (websocket + polling paths), with **`MAX_ENTRY_ATTEMPTS = 2` — i.e. exactly ONE retry, the same for every name.** It has fired **7 times, ALL PAPER, ALL in early May 2026 (last 05-11), and NEVER on live money.** ▶ **SO HIS IDEA MAPS ONTO AN EXISTING KNOB:** `MAX_ENTRY_ATTEMPTS` is a flat constant today; he is asking for it to be **confidence-scaled** — more retries when we believe it is a real EP, one when we do not. ▶ **NEXT STEPS: (1)** re-price re-entry on the real-EP fixture only; **(2)** measure what the live day-1 retry has actually done in its 7 paper firings; **(3)** design the confidence→retry-count mapping. ⚖ Retry count is entry discipline = THE LINE; research and propose, never flip. Then re-state flip / hold / pre-open-only to the operator. ⚠ Admission is entry discipline = THE LINE; this task produces a RECOMMENDATION, never a flip. Registry predicate: `rt_admission_recut_post_2r_exits` in `data_gated_reviews.yaml`. >> 🔴 **FIRST LIKELY FALSE BLOCK ON THE LIVE ENTRY GATE — SE, 2026-08-11, operator-spotted.** Yesterday this line's evidence said the entry-time real-time gap gate had fired 4 times with 4 CORRECT blocks. Today it produced a 5th that looks wrong. **SE was skipped at 09:31:10 with `setup:gap_below_floor: rt 9.2% < 10% floor (alert said 10.6%, last $125.36 vs prev close $114.80)` — and by 09:35 SE traded $126.83, i.e. +10.5%, back above the floor.** Operator: *"SE dropped below 10% but reclaimed quickly."* ⚠ **THE MECHANISM, stated neutrally:** the gate is a SINGLE point-in-time sample taken in the first seconds of the session, the noisiest moment of the day. A name that dips through the floor for one tick is rejected for the whole session with no re-look. That is a property of the design, not a bug — but it means the gate's block rate and its FALSE-block rate are different numbers and only one of them has ever been counted. ✅ **ADDED TO THIS TASK'S DoD:** when the 08-31 re-cut runs, also report the gate's blocks split into (a) blocked and stayed below the floor — a correct save, and (b) blocked and reclaimed the floor within the ORB window — a candidate false block, with what the name did afterwards. A gate whose saves are counted and whose misses are not cannot be evaluated. ⚠ Entry discipline = THE LINE: this is MEASUREMENT being added, no threshold and no re-check behaviour is proposed here. >> 🔴🔴 **TWST 2026-08-19 IS THE CLEANEST CASE THIS TASK WILL EVER GET — and it is NOT a window problem, it is the AUTHORITY toggle.** Operator: *"we had alert on TWST EP but came too late… This is a stock i would've liked to enter but our system timing prevented it."* **THE TRACE, from `mi_audit_log`:** `ep_rt_floor_flip_up` at **09:30:04** — **rt_gap 10.69%, delayed_gap 9.34%.** Real time was ALREADY over the floor four seconds into the session. But every RT row logs `authoritative: false`, so **the DELAYED number decided and it was below the floor.** Admission finally fired at **09:45:11**, when the delayed feed caught up to 10.42% — by which time rt_gap was 19.46%. ⏱ **The ORB submission window is 09:31-09:44 ET. Admission at 09:45:11 is eleven seconds past it** → `WINDOW_OUT_OF_ORB` (recorded 09:55). **We did not miss TWST because the window is too short. We missed it because we spent the window waiting for a slower feed to agree with one we already had.** 🔑 **`ep_rt_universe_authoritative` HAS NO ROW — it defaults OFF.** `ep_rt_gap_down_authoritative`, `ep_rt_entry_gap_recheck` and `ep_rt_sustain_enabled` are all ON — **so real time is already trusted to REMOVE a candidate (gap-down) and to RE-CHECK one at entry, but not to ADMIT one.** That asymmetry is the whole defect, and TWST is it costing a trade he wanted. ⚠ **THIS ALSO NARROWS MY OWN 08-18 CONCLUSION.** The `490_delayed_screen_cost` read found the delayed screen was NOT costing us EPs — but it measured NEVER-ALERTED names and their outcomes. **TWST alerted, and was 15 minutes late.** A different population and a different failure mode; that study does not cover this and must not be cited against it. ▶ **The 08-31 HOLD date is now questionable under P6 (bias for action): the cost of waiting is a named, wanted trade already lost.** ⚖ Admission authority is entry discipline = THE LINE — his ruling, and the ONE thing to price first is what `ep_rt_universe_authoritative=on` would have admitted historically, winners AND losers (P5). >> 🔑 **OPERATOR CORRECTION 2026-08-19 — I FRAMED THIS AS A DATE AND IT NEVER WAS.** His words: *"hold date for what? to flip to real-time? The date is not the issue, the issue goes back to what we just discuss, how good is our selection downstream if we admit more because realtime will admit a lot more, unfortunately in this case, we missed a potential winner."* **The gate on flipping `ep_rt_universe_authoritative` is P9, not the calendar: real time admits a LOT more, and whether that is safe depends entirely on whether downstream selection can sort the bigger pool.** A date cannot answer that; only a measured ranker can. **Re-dating this to 08-22 is bookkeeping — the real unblock condition is below and it is not a date.** ▶ **THE ACTUAL UNBLOCK CONDITION: the rank shadow demonstrates it can rank the larger pool.** It became a RUNNING read on 08-18 (fires from n=1 with confidence stated, no longer a one-shot in October) and reports BOTH directions per P5 — so it can answer this progressively rather than on a threshold date. **Until it does, loosening admission just moves the problem: more candidates competing for the same 5 slots on a ranker we have not validated out-of-sample.** ⚖ **AND THE COST OF WAITING IS NOW NAMED, not theoretical: TWST 2026-08-19.** Real time read 10.69% at 09:30:04; the delayed feed said 9.34%; admission fired 09:45:11, eleven seconds after the ORB window shut. **A trade he wanted, lost to feed latency, not to a criterion.** That is the price of the current asymmetry and it should be weighed against the dilution risk, not ignored. 📌 **SAME SHAPE AS THE GAP FLOOR, and he resolved that one the same way:** priced both options, took the SMALLER (9.0% not 8.0%) precisely because downstream selectivity is unproven. **Expect the same logic here — a partial or staged flip is more defensible than a full one until the ranker earns it.**

So it is **0/10**, not nearly-there. Note the shape: only 3 of 476 rows are MODERATE at all and every one is themeless — like #310, this gate may be structurally slow rather than merely early. Earliest review date is 2026-09-15 regardless. >> 🔑 **GATE RE-POINTED + PREMISE TESTED 2026-07-26 (operator ruled option b).** ROOT CAUSE of the 0/10 stall, measured: (1) MODERATE was only instrumented from **2026-07-15** (11 days of data); (2) **87% of EP alerts genuinely have NO theme — and that is CORRECT, not under-detection**: of 417 `themeless_flag=true` rows, only **1** had its ticker in a live theme within 3 days. So themed-MODERATE = rare × rare ≈ **0.65/month → ~15 MONTHS to reach 10.** Nothing to fix; the GATE was set assuming a rate that does not exist. >> **So we tested the boost's PREMISE on the population that DOES exist (themed HIGH n=59 vs themeless HIGH n=414, fwd-5d from alert-date close): themed mean +0.50% / median −1.70% / 44% win vs themeless mean −0.60% / median −0.43% / 46% win.** Better mean, WORSE median, slightly lower win-rate → **FAILS the both-measures bar** (the same profile as the #170 re-setup finding: thin right tail, worse typical case). ⚠ **Evidence AGAINST, not proof:** this tests CORRELATION (does theme predict edge?) on an already-selected HIGH cohort, whereas the boost is designed for the MARGIN (lift themed MODERATE → HIGH). Theme signal can be redundant among HIGHs and still matter at the margin — but there is no themed-MODERATE population to check that on, and won't be for ~15 months. Also close-to-close fwd, which understates intraday. **CONSEQUENCE: do NOT flip #335 on current evidence**; the premise does not clear its own bar. And #301/#329 no longer wait on a 15-month gate — they decouple with a 'no' rather than parking indefinitely. >> ⛔ **CONCLUSION RETRACTED SAME DAY — my 'do NOT flip' read does not stand (operator, 3 grounds, all correct).** (a) **My themeless check was CIRCULAR.** I verified `themeless_flag` matches `mi_themes` and concluded 87% themeless is 'correct, not under-detection'. That only proves our DETECTOR AGREES WITH ITSELF. **Not detecting a theme ≠ no theme exists** — which is exactly why the EP signal is meant to FEED BACK INTO theme discovery, not merely consume it. The 15-month projection inherits that error. (b) **REGIME CONFOUND**: the themed-HIGH test ran over a window that is mostly Correcting/Choppy, when hot/themed names are hit HARDEST — measuring theme value in a correction is biased against themes by construction. (c) **I evaluated the wrong thing.** I tested themes on ONE narrow use (the MODERATE→HIGH grade boost) and called the axis marginal. The real value is PORTFOLIO-LEVEL: **boost capital allocation toward hot areas · arbitrate competing EPs for limited slots · expand slots for a strong theme.** Those justify getting the rubric right independent of today's marginal grade lift. **NET: the themed-HIGH result (mean +0.50 / median −1.70 vs themeless −0.60 / −0.43) stands as a DATA POINT, not a verdict — it is regime-confounded, detector-limited, and scoped to the least valuable use of the axis.** #335's flip decision stays OPEN. >> 🔧 **STATUS CORRECTED 2026-08-09 — `deployed` was wrong: the flip was NEVER TURNED ON.** The line's own last words are *"flip decision stays OPEN"*, and `composite_authority` has never been enabled, so there is nothing shipped in production to verify. It was a **pending operator decision wearing a deployed status** — exactly the shape that makes a headline lie. **Now `pending`.** ▶ CONCRETE CONDITION, and it is his not mine: the eval result goes to the operator, he rules flip / don't flip. If he rules DON'T, this closes as decided — a ruling is a legitimate close, an unflipped toggle is not a verify. [The Real EP Plan (ep_profitability_program.md) — Selection/ranking surface]

## Operational safety / hardening
- #583 | 2026-08-27 | pending | 🐛 **`mi_ep_missed_outcomes` never re-validates rows outside its 30-day refresh window, so a categorisation FIX leaves the old rows lying — and they silently corrupt every gate study built on the table.** Found 2026-08-22 by the #577 Card B agent. 📉 **THE DAMAGE, verified in prod:** `high_unentered` holds **287 of 298 rows stale** (oldest 2026-02-11, months before every other skip_category begins) and `window_missed` **32 of 57**. They are orphaned rows from a June replay batch that a later fix excluded going forward but never pruned retroactively. 🔑 **WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND THOSE TWO:** my 08-21 gate-ranking table credited both categories with 5 ≥100%-winners each; the true count for both is **ZERO**. Any analysis that reads this table — and #556, #557 and both #577 cards all do — inherits the error for any category with rows older than the refresh window. **This is a silent-corruption class, not a cosmetic one.** ▶ **BUILD:** (1) prune or re-validate rows that fall outside the refresh window when their categorisation logic changes; (2) a guard so a stale row cannot be counted as a live skip; (3) re-state the affected counts. ⚖ **NOT a strategy question — a data-integrity defect, so no operator sign-off is needed, only scheduling.** **DoD:** the table's counts match what the CURRENT categorisation logic would produce, and a test pins that a stale row is excluded. [b1]
- #582 | 2026-08-26 | pending | **`theme_synthesis` has NO truncation guard — a cut response parses as "0 cohorts", the exact shape that made `theme_split` tell us a lie.** Found 2026-08-21 by the #575 card. 🔑 **THE PRECEDENT IS THE ARGUMENT:** on 2026-08-10 a truncated `theme_split` response parsed as an affirmative *"already coherent"* — the engine did not just lose a call, it acted on a fabricated verdict. `theme_synthesis` has the same shape: no `is_truncated` check, so a response cut mid-JSON degrades to an empty cohort list that reads as a legitimate "nothing emerging tonight". 🟢 **Risk is much LOWER since #575** (thinking is now disabled on this caller, so it has far more room), **but lower is not zero** and the failure is silent by construction. ▶ **BUILD:** an `is_truncated` guard on the `theme_synthesis` response — on `stop_reason=max_tokens` raise or return an explicit error, never an empty result. Mirror what `theme_split` already does. ▶ **DoD:** a cut `theme_synthesis` response produces a loud audit row, not a silent zero; a test pins it. Themes touch no money, so this ships full. [b1]
- #576 | 2026-08-24 | deployed | 🔴 **TWO FALSE MONEY-PATH ALERTS IN ONE MORNING — the operator: *"it's very confusing and adds noise"*.** Both fired 2026-08-19 on MRNA, both were WRONG, and neither cost money — which is exactly why they are corrosive: a money-path alert that cries wolf trains him to discount the ones that matter. >> ✅ **BOTH FIXED 2026-08-19.** ⚠ **The card FLAGGED that it could not confirm which producer actually fired, and it was right to — I checked and the answer changes nothing but was worth proving:** `mi_live_trades` holds exactly ONE MRNA row today (id 374, `filled`, skip_reason NULL) and **ZERO rows anywhere carry a max_positions skip**. So the message did NOT come from a DB skip_reason. **The digest is built from IN-MEMORY `results` in `live_tracker.py:533`**, and it already excludes `WINDOW_DUPLICATE` (#475, the dual bar_stream/cron ORB trigger). **The defect is ORDERING: on the duplicate path the CAP check fired BEFORE the duplicate branch, so the sibling reported `block:max_positions` instead of the excluded `WINDOW_DUPLICATE`.** The card's fix is therefore in the right place — a same-key `EXISTS` before the cap short-circuit in `entry_pipeline.py` STEP 6, letting the INSERT's own `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` resolve it silently. **A real cap block for a DIFFERENT ticker is untouched.** **(b) The naked branch now requires TWO independently-written immutable audit rows** — `partial_exit_breakeven_armed` (written only AFTER the broker confirms the new stop is live) AND `partial_exit_stop_telegram_pending` — naming the SAME trade_id, SAME new_stop_id, and a price within a cent. 🔑 **The card's FIRST draft re-read `mi_live_trades` and the advisor caught that this same handler NULLs that exact column moments earlier — it would have been inert on the very incident it targets.** Two rows this handler never writes cannot be self-clobbered. ✅ **A genuinely naked position still alarms** — pinned by `test_genuinely_naked_position_still_alarms_with_no_evidence`; any gap (one row missing, wrong trade, mismatched id or price, failed read, malformed JSON) speaks. Mutations: remove the self-race check → reproduces the exact pre-fix text; force the naked gate true → 7 of 9 still-alarms tests go red. Suite 5564/7, 12 new tests. ⚠ **SIDE EFFECT worth knowing: #461's `cap_recheck_blocked` verify counter no longer fires for a self-race loser** — correct, since that is a different event, but flag it to anyone watching that count. ▶ **VERIFY-LIVE 2026-08-20 = the next day a duplicate ORB trigger and a +2R partial both occur: NO 'max positions' line for a name in the book, and NO 'Position unprotected' during a normal carve-out.** ➕ **THIRD CONFUSING MONEY-PATH ALERT, 2026-08-20 (RARE) — operator asked *"which is the reason it's not entered?"*, which is the tell.** He got TWO messages for ONE name with DIFFERENT account labels and DIFFERENT reasons: `📄 PAPER ⚠️ RARE ORB skipped — No opening bar (3 retries exhausted)` and `💰 LIVE-$ ⏭️ ORB skips — Gap retreated below the 10% floor (rt 5.4% < 9%)`. **GROUND TRUTH:** `mi_live_trades` id 378 holds exactly ONE row — `account_mode=live`, `infra:no_bar: 3 retries exhausted`, 09:31:20 ET. The gap-retreat skip has NO row at all: the digest is built from the ORB monitor's IN-MEMORY `results` (`live_tracker.py:522-546`), not from the DB, so a later cron pass can report a skip that was never persisted. ⚠ **RULED OUT:** the label is NOT the fail-open mode-resolve degrading to the legacy prefix — `strategy_mode_resolve_error` fired ZERO times today (checked). So the PAPER prefix is either a genuine second strategy also evaluating RARE on paper, or a real mislabel on a live-money surface. **UNRESOLVED — that is the bit to fix.** ✅ **SHIPPED alongside:** `skip_reasons.py:98` said *'Gap retreated below the 10% floor'* while the floor has been **9.0%** since 2026-08-19 — a stale hardcoded number on a money-path message, now reads 'the entry floor'. ⚠ **NOT YET DEPLOYED** — `broker/` runs on apollo-execution, needs `deploy.sh execution`. 🔬 **ROOT-CAUSED 2026-08-20 (and it CORRECTS the read above — no entry was lost):** the execution log settles it. Two of the five cases are now this shape. >> ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-17 (operator-directed: *"we need to tighten up what gets surfaced and not waste time reviewing what's not ready"*).** TWO detectors in `data_gated_reviews.py`, both static, no DB: `is_date_fire_predicate` (no FROM clause = a calendar dressed as SQL) and `find_population_mismatch` (the predicate's table has a discriminating column — `signal_type`/`account_mode`/`entry_mode` — and the predicate does not filter it). Ready items now render `[date-only — not evidence-gated]` instead of `[ripe Nd]`, and a mismatch shows a LIVE CATEGORY BREAKDOWN (`magna53=9, 9m_day2=8`) so it is visible without running the review. (2) my `stop_too_wide` `last_run_note` had been pasted onto the WRONG entry (`gap_alignment_331_accrual`) and was moved. 🔴 **AND IT CAUGHT A THIRD, AFTER I THOUGHT I HAD FIXED IT:** scoped to `magna53` the predicate still did not filter `account_mode`, and the 9 rows are **4 live + 5 paper**. Scope is now written out in the entry as a DECISION (paper rejections are valid here — a skip never became a position, so the forward outcome is account-independent) rather than an accident. ⚠ **NOT built, deliberately:** a hard pre-commit gate on population-mismatch (18 existing flags would block every commit — kept informational in the lint); a closed-task-marks-review hook (nothing calls it); a generalized off-schema-field detector (the one live instance was fixed by hand; the class is recorded as residual). But the mismatch detector's coverage is bounded entirely by a HAND-MAINTAINED `table -> [column]` dict, verified once against the prod schema on 08-17 with **no ongoing link to it**. A new table, or a new discriminating column on an existing table, gets SILENTLY ZERO coverage — and `tests/test_review_readiness_sanity.py` only exercises hand-written SQL against the existing dict, never against real schema, so nothing signals the dict is stale. Of today's two tightenings only the `COUNT(DISTINCT)` exemption was a general rule; dropping `strategy_id` was hand-editing one entry after a human caught it. **The deeper form: have each YAML entry DECLARE the table.column its predicate must filter — written once by whoever already knows the population — and make the check declared-vs-mentioned, which fails LOUD on a missing declaration instead of degrading silently.** ▶ Filed as the next increment; do not keep hand-patching the dict. >> 📋 **VERIFY 2026-08-19 — ONE OF THREE OBSERVABLES CONFIRMED IN PROD; NOT CLOSING ON A PARTIAL.** Ran `check_pending_reviews()` live: 15 ready items. ✅ **The population-mismatch half WORKS on real data** — 3 entries flagged with genuine breakdowns: `kill_scale_bands_quarterly_review` (`mi_live_trades.signal_type` → magna53=22), `orb_5m_reentry_hybrid_replay` (`mi_live_trades.account_mode`), `exit_tune_cohort_review` (two columns). That is the mismatch class that let a MAGNA53 review fire on 9m_day2 rows, now visible BEFORE anyone runs a review. ⚠ **The other two labels could not be observed, and that is a property of the data, not a defect:** `date_fire` returned [] because the one honest date-fire entry (the 620/minute-pull park) has `earliest_review_date` 2026-09-15 and is not READY yet; `last_run_inconclusive_on` returned [] because `stop_too_wide_outcome_cohort` carries it but dropped out of READY once its predicate was corrected to magna53-only (9 < threshold 10). **Both are correct behaviour — the labels have nothing to label.** ▶ **Re-dated to 2026-08-23 (Sunday) — the digest run is when a date-fire item can actually surface. Do NOT close on the mismatch half alone.**

- #580 | 2026-08-28 | pending | **Theme strength must be reported WITH breadth, and the one-sided trim must be measured before it is trusted — the operator found `/theme` ranking biotech #2 while the dashboard ranked it #3 (2026-08-20) and ruled that neither surface is the answer: *"you named flaws in both… the solution is not one or the other, but a 3rd better option."* He is right.** 🔎 **THE TWO SURFACES DIVERGE FOR THREE REASONS, all confirmed in code:** (a) `/theme` (`agent.py:4822` → `briefing.py:549` `_compute_scored_themes`) RECOMPUTES from current constituent RS, while `dashboard/theme_rank_evolution.py` reads the STORED `mi_themes.score` written by the previous nightly run; (b) `/theme` excludes Fading, the dashboard applies no Fading/Retired filter; (c) the dashboard ranks inside a WEEKLY bucket (`compute_weekly_rank`, `drop_duplicates(['week_start','name'], keep='last')`), so a Monday row can rank against a Thursday row. Verified against prod the same day: both produce an identical top-5 once 'latest row per name' is applied, so the Retired gap (#214) was NOT the active cause — freshness + Fading + bucketing are. 🚩 **THE DEFECT UNDERNEATH BOTH: a theme is reduced to ONE averaged number, and that average is computed by a ONE-SIDED trim.** `constants.trimmed_mean` sorts ascending and drops the lowest N (`sorted_vals[drop:]`) — the top is NEVER trimmed. Its docstring claims outlier resistance, but a real trimmed mean cuts both tails; this one only ever deletes losers, so every theme scores structurally high, always in the same direction. **At our actual basket sizes it is not 20%:** the live top themes hold 3, 4, 5 and 3 stocks (prod, 2026-08-19), so a 3-stock theme drops 1 of 3 — 33% — and the 'score' is a two-stock average. A theme carried by two names and a theme where eight of ten participate are indistinguishable. 📉 **BREADTH IS ALREADY COMPUTED AND ALREADY LOAD-BEARING — it is simply never surfaced.** `mi_themes.pct_above_20sma` is written by `theme_engine.py:3179/3338` and already FORCES a theme to Fading below `_BREADTH_DECAY_THRESHOLD` (`theme_engine.py:3285`). ⚠ **But it is NULL on 29 of 120 rows (2026-08-19) — including all four of the top-scoring themes.** The newest, hottest themes are exactly the ones with no breadth number, which is the population where it matters most; root-cause that before surfacing it, or the new column renders blank precisely when he looks. ✅ **BUILD (three pieces, in this order):** (1) **Surface breadth next to strength** on `/theme` and on the dashboard — small, the number already exists and is stored; (2) **fix the NULL-on-new-theme gap** so the top themes carry a breadth figure (`theme_engine.py:6233` sets None on the Retired path; find the new-theme path that does the same); (3) **MEASURE the trim before changing it** — do NOT edit it blind: `trimmed_mean` has SEVEN call sites across `briefing.py` (4), `theme_engine.py` (2 — including the engine's own `momentum`, which drives stage decisions) and `theme_ecosystems.py` (2), so a change moves engine stage transitions and ecosystem scores, not just a display. Produce a $0 read-only before/after on stored cohorts: score with trim vs plain mean vs both-tails trim, and report how much rank order actually moves. 🏗 **ONE COMPUTATION, TWO LABELLED SURFACES.** `dashboard/theme_rank_evolution.py` is a standalone streamlit+psycopg2 process with NO imports from `agents/`, so 'shared' cannot mean a shared import — it means the engine WRITES the canonical number and both surfaces READ it, with `/theme` labelled 'where strength is now' and the dashboard labelled 'what the engine acted on'. Today they are two unlabelled rankings, which is why a disagreement reads as a bug. 🧭 **NORTH STAR:** subtle RS → early theme → buy before mainstream (memory `theme-north-star-early-rs-before-mainstream`). Breadth is what separates a real early theme from two stocks with a story — the exact discrimination the north star needs. ⛔ **NOT before the weekend EP work** (P10: EP profitability before new setups). **DoD:** `/theme` and the dashboard both show strength + breadth from the same stored number; no NULL breadth on any non-Retired theme; a written before/after showing what the trim was doing to rank order, with a ruling on whether it stays. ➕ **A THIRD DISAGREEING COUNT, found 2026-08-21 — same defect, new surface.** The L2 anomaly alert reported `theme_count_active = 159` against a 30d median of 122, while `mi_themes` holds **113 non-Retired rows for that date**. The alert counts DISTINCT NAMES seen in the last 7 days (`get_active_themes(stale_after_days=7)`); the table counts today's rows. Neither is wrong; they answer different questions and **both are labelled 'active themes'**, so the alert fires on a definitional gap rather than a real jump. ⚠ The underlying row count IS drifting up on its own (100 → 107 → 120 → 127 → 128 over five days) — that is worth a look, but it is not what the alert measured. ▶ Fold this surface into the same fix: one computation, each surface labelled with the question it answers.

It was built to answer the bump chart's crossing-lines problem (#315 R2) and it does fix the crossings — but it fails for a different reason. 📊 **WHY, from the screenshot:** (a) almost all the ink is the BORING case — the thick horizontal ribbons are cohorts STAYING in their band — while promotions are hairline threads; (b) every ribbon is the same pale green, so nothing signals which move matters; (c) the band labels (Top 5 / 6-15 / 16-30 / Outside) render ON TOP of the ribbons and are half unreadable; (d) 4 bands x 11 weeks is far too many ribbons to trace one cohort through. ⚠ **THE REAL DEFECT IS NOT COSMETIC — it answers the wrong question.** It reports HOW MANY cohorts changed band. - #553 | 2026-08-25 | deployed | **Theme cohort-matching produces FALSE MERGES — a small theme inside a larger unrelated one is absorbed regardless of similarity** (found 2026-08-08 when the operator asked *"heatmap is the exact same as grid?"* — measuring that killed the heatmap view and exposed this underneath). ROOT CAUSE in `portfolio-app2/theme_data.py::dedup_themes`: the match test is CONTAINMENT of the smaller set (`|S ∩ L| / |S| >= 0.50`) with Jaccard used only as a TIE-BREAK, never as a gate. So a 2-3 member theme wholly inside a larger unrelated theme merges at containment 1.00 however different the cohorts are — and because merges CHAIN (A~B, B~C → A+B+C), unrelated themes collapse onto one identity. EVIDENCE (real snapshot): 'Satellite Mobile & IoT Connectivity' absorbed 'U.S. Defense Primes & Aerospace' (GD/LMT/NOC/RTX); 'Niche Specialty Chemicals' fused with 'IP Licensing & Ad-Tech Royalty Software'; a fertilizer→specialty-chem→engineered-polymer chain became one row. ⚠ **Rank Flow (live) is built on this identity** and inherits it — tolerable only because it aggregates into 4 rank bands, so a mis-key shifts a ribbon's thickness rather than mislabeling a row. Bump Chart + Forward Returns use it too, and Forward Returns' cohort counts depend on it. FIX DIRECTION (not yet decided): require BOTH a containment floor AND a Jaccard floor rather than containment alone; cap the size ratio on the subset path; and stop chain-merging (a merge target that has itself already absorbed a different cohort should not keep absorbing). ⚠ Same family as apollo-side theme churn (#476 name/member instability, #471 re-granularization) but this is the DASHBOARD's own matcher, a separate code path — do not conflate. DoD = the 3 evidenced false merges no longer merge + a regression test pinning each + the true-duplicate defense pair (Spending Surge / Contract Surge, both {PLTR,TSAT,VOYG,AMRC}) STILL merges + Rank Flow re-verified. [b0] >> ✅ **FIXED + PUSHED 2026-08-08** (operator: *"fix it"*). All 3 false merges verified gone by running the OLD and NEW matchers SIDE BY SIDE on the same snapshot (before=merged→after=separate on each); the true defence duplicate still merges. 34/311 → 23/332 merged cohorts. **3 mechanisms, only ONE was a suspect I handed over**: (a) satellite/defence was cross-day FIRST CONTACT — a brand-new name claiming a cohort it had never touched at Jaccard 0.667; (b) chemicals/ad-tech was the `min(min_shared, denom)` relaxation letting a 2-of-2 match through on a 2-ticker signature; (c) the chain was NOT one bad hop — every day-to-day step was individually plausible (0.7-1.0) while cumulative drift walked a 5-ticker cohort to an unrelated 15-ticker basket. FIXES: an **anchor set** (cohort freezes its tickers at creation; later matches must clear the bar against THAT, not just the drifted latest) — this is what actually stops chaining; a **0.70 first-contact floor** (board-wide ablation, sits between the bad 0.667 and genuine renames at 0.714/0.778); relaxation removed; `dedup_themes` gains an OPTIONAL `jaccard_floor` (default None). **GRID PROVEN UNCHANGED** — I re-verified the strongest check myself: `get_weekly_grid()` CSV sha256 identical before/after (ffea9cd2d93aea54). ⚠ My own first acceptance test was too coarse ('do these names ever share a cohort') and nearly rejected a correct fix — the remaining overlaps are genuine (83%, and near-identical 15-ticker sets). RESIDUAL left deliberately: a broad 'Oil & Gas' absorbs 3 same-day strict-subset sub-themes — a granularity loss, not a wrong-cohort error, and the same question #471 owns. VERIFY-DUE 8/10: operator confirms Rank Flow reads correctly on the live URL. >> 🔭 **NEXT STEP IDENTIFIED — the matcher needs a MODEL, not a tenth guard** (/simplify altitude + simplification reviews, 2026-08-08, both empirical). The anchor set is the right instinct — it is the first thing giving a cohort an ORIGIN independent of its drifted state — but it is expressed as one more `if…continue` in a greedy pairwise chain that now stacks NINE constraints on a single match decision, FOUR added in one commit, none removing an earlier one. `_FIRST_CONTACT_THRESHOLD=0.70` sits in a **0.014-wide margin** between the bad case (0.667) and the nearest good one (0.714) on N=2 — threshold-stacking that has run out of room. The receipts are this task's own two documented residuals. **Deeper fix = cohort identity as a real model** (origin + a bounded drift budget, or clustering over the full time series instead of greedy day-by-day chaining) — a REWRITE of `canonicalize_themes`, not a patch. ➡ **NOW ITS OWN LINE — #555** (operator authorised the carryover 2026-08-08). It was held here only while the board sat at its ceiling; it is a rewrite, not a continuation of this patch, and it does not belong on this line. ✅ Also empirically CONFIRMED tonight by patching each knob out and diffing the whole dataset: 5 of 6 knobs + the anchor rule are each independently load-bearing (removing any one reproduces a real false merge); only `_SIZE_RATIO_CAP` is inert (Jaccard≥0.50 already implies ratio≤2.0). >> ❌ **VERIFY FAILED 2026-08-10 — the DoD says 'the 3 evidenced false merges no longer merge' and, run against tonight's live snapshot, ALL THREE STILL MERGE in the March-April portion of the series.** Measured with `get_canonical_themes()` on `apollo_themes_snapshot.json` (exported 08-10 18:53): 'Satellite Mobile & IoT Connectivity Services' 03-25..04-08 (9 rows) canonicalizes to **'U.S. Defense Primes & Aerospace'** · 'Niche Specialty Chemicals & Industrial Intermediates' 03-24..03-27 (3 rows) to **'Engineered Polymers & High-Performance Acetyl Chemicals'** · 'Nylon & Engineered Polymer Intermediates' 04-02..04-08 (3 rows) to **'Agricultural Commodities & Agri-Business'**. ✅ **AND THE FIX IS REAL WHERE IT COUNTS:** every one of those same raw names is correctly STANDALONE from mid-April onward (satellite 04-16..05-07, chemicals 04-13..05-14, polymers 04-14..06-12). So the 08-08 side-by-side check was not wrong — it was NARROW: it compared matchers on specific pair-instances, not across the whole 19-week series, and the anchor rule cannot retroactively unpick a cohort claimed before it existed. ⚠ **The dashboard's default windows reach back into the affected range**, so this is visible, not academic. ⚠ **ALSO INCONSISTENT — the TRUE duplicate:** 'U.S. Government/Defense Spending Surge' merges into 'Contract Surge' on 08-04 but stands alone on 08-05 — the pair the DoD requires to KEEP merging does so on one of its two days. ➡ **THIS IS #555's CASE, not a tenth guard.** Re-dated behind the identity rewrite (#555, ETA 08-22); re-run exactly this check afterwards and close only if all three stay separate ACROSS THE FULL SERIES and the true duplicate merges on every day it appears. [ok:2026-08-10→2026-08-25 — verify RAN and FAILED on live data; not a re-date for want of effort, and the failing evidence is recorded above] >> 🔴 **THE INVERSE DEFECT, FOUND LIVE 2026-08-18 — cohort matching also produces FALSE NON-MERGES, and this task only covers false merges.** The theme engine ran TWICE tonight (17:00:38 and 17:05:36 ET — caused by MY execution deploy inside the 17:00-21:00 nightly-chain blackout; see [[deploy-timing-avoid-market-windows]]). The two runs produced **two live themes with IDENTICAL membership under different names**: `Reinsurance & Specialty Insurance Holding Carriers` (EG RNR CINF ORI L, 21:14:34) and `P&C Insurance & Reinsurance Holding Companies` (CINF EG L ORI RNR, 21:15:59) — the same five tickers, 85 seconds apart. Both rendered in the SAME digest, including twice in the deterioration section with the same members listed. **ROOT CAUSE: dedup/merge matches on NAME, not on MEMBERSHIP.** Any two runs that word a cohort differently produce coexisting duplicates, and the operator sees both. ✅ **CLEANED 2026-08-18 on his instruction (*"clean it up"*): row id 6822 deleted, the NEWER of the pair, older kept. Full table backed up first to `apollo@87.99.134.162:/home/apollo/mi_themes_backup_20260818.csv` (108 rows). Verified after: 107 themes, ZERO exact-duplicate pairs remaining.** ⚠ **NOT deleted, and NOT the same thing — three CONTAINMENT pairs where the smaller theme is 100%% inside the larger:** `Natural Gas E&P Producers` (5) inside `Independent Oil Refiners` (42) · `South African Gold & PGM Mining Producers` (4) inside `Gold & Precious Metals Miners Rotation` (17) · `Americas-Focused Mid-Tier Gold & Silver Producers` (3) inside `Latin American Silver & Gold Mining Operators` (5). A sub-theme inside a broad one is LEGITIMATE under the parent-child model (#505) — deleting those would be over-reach. But the Oil-Refiners one looks like a genuine mis-name worth a look. ▶ **DoD ADD: dedup must key on MEMBERSHIP (a sorted ticker set), not the name** — exact-set matches merge, and high-overlap pairs route to the parent-child path rather than coexisting. >> 📌 **THE DEPLOY WINDOW, MAPPED FROM THE SCHEDULER (2026-08-18, operator asked: *"you can deploy after 8 or 9PM PT, nothing is running then, right?"* — YES).** Every evening/overnight job, read from `scheduler.py`: **18:00 ET** crypto_nightly_ingest · **18:05 ET** theme_synthesis · **18:30 ET** minute_volume_curves_refresh · **19:00 ET** crypto_category_refresh · **21:00 ET** evening_position_backstop · then NOTHING until **02:00 ET** baseline_refresh · 06:00 ET theme_round_trip_validator. ▶ **SAFE WINDOW ≈ 18:15-22:45 PT (21:15-01:45 ET)** — his 8-9 PM PT is comfortably inside it. ⚠ The existing blackouts stand: 09:25-10:05 ET (ORB/money), 16:00-17:00 ET (EOD digest), and 17:00-21:00 ET is now shown to be the NIGHTLY CHAIN — which is what my 17:05 ET deploy tonight clipped, re-firing the theme engine and creating the duplicate cohort. [[deploy-timing-avoid-market-windows]] >> ⚠ **AND A RATCHET WORTH NAMING (simplify review 2026-08-18), on the theme_discovery batch size:** each re-derivation starts from a **CENSORED floor** — once a batch hits the cap, `8000/N` is a lower bound and the true demand is unobservable from above. EVIDENCE it cannot be tuned further: #553's own two documented residuals (a broad 'Oil & Gas' absorbing 3 same-day strict subsets at exactly Jaccard 0.50; a stray later row re-attaching to a cohort it was split from) — **ticker-only matching cannot distinguish a re-attachment from a genuine rename, which is a structural limit, not a threshold.** DIRECTION (decide with evidence, do not pre-commit): cohort identity as origin + a bounded DRIFT BUDGET, or clustering over the FULL time series instead of greedy day-by-day chaining. ✅ WHAT MUST SURVIVE (regression set already exists from #553): 3 false merges stay split, the {PLTR,TSAT,VOYG,AMRC} defence duplicate stays merged, Grid stays byte-identical at **ALL 21 slider values** (weeks 4-24 — a single-value hash is NOT sufficient, advisor 8/08), 332 cohorts as the reference point. ⚠ Consumers: Rank Flow, Bump Chart, Forward Returns (11% of its events currently rest on a merged cohort). ⚠ Related but DISTINCT: #471/#476 are the apollo-side theme engine; this is the DASHBOARD's own matcher. DoD = the regression set green + the two residuals resolved or explicitly accepted with reasons + knob count DOWN, not up. [b0]
- #554 | 2026-08-25 | pending | **19 call sites take `MAX(score_date)` from `mi_stock_scores` and can silently read a 1-row day** (root-caused 2026-08-08 while fixing the dashboard export). MECHANISM: `rs_engine.py:587 score_single_ticker` stamps its ONE on-demand row with `et_today()` — the CALENDAR date, not `last_trading_day()` — so a single lookup of a held name on a non-trading day creates a `score_date` whose entire population is that one row (tonight: FIGS, Sat 2026-08-08). A real nightly run writes ~2,400. EVIDENCE it recurs: exactly ONE row on 2026-05-30, 07-05, 07-12, 08-08 — all Saturdays, ~monthly since May. **Already fixed for the ONE consumer that provably fired** (`scripts/export_theme_snapshot.sql`, display-only, now takes the newest date with ≥50% of the trailing-10-day median count; verified on prod 0 → 725 rows). ⚠ STILL EXPOSED: `theme_engine.py:2137` + `:2301` (RS reads on the theme path), `agent.py:5283` (Telegram-facing, runs whenever he asks — INCLUDING weekends), plus ~15 in `db.py`/scripts. The theme engine dodges this by running mon-fri — **luck, not design**. TWO candidate fixes, decide with evidence: (a) a shared `latest_complete_score_date()` helper the call sites use, or (b) `score_single_ticker` dates by `last_trading_day()`. ⚠ (b) changes RS row semantics on a DETECTION input — classify BUG-FIX vs criteria-change before invoking CHANGE_PROCESS [[classify-before-applying-evidence-gates]]. DoD = a partial/stray day can no longer be selected by any consumer + a regression test using the real 1-row-Saturday shape. [b2] >> ⛔ **DO NOT 'FIX' THIS BY SWAPPING THE DATE — it would DESTROY data** (found by the 2026-08-08 /simplify altitude review, which traced the write site properly). `score_single_ticker` ALREADY computes the right date — `use_date` at rs_engine.py:634, the most recent populated score_date within 5 days — and uses it for the ranking distribution; only the WRITE at :680 stamps `today` instead. But `upsert_stock_score` (db.py:2989-3011) does `ON CONFLICT (ticker, score_date) DO UPDATE SET ... sector=EXCLUDED.sector, adv_20=EXCLUDED.adv_20, market_cap=EXCLUDED.market_cap`, and the on-demand record hardcodes all three to None (rs_engine.py:686-688). So a naive `today`→`use_date` swap makes ONE single-ticker lookup OVERWRITE that ticker's nightly row and NULL its sector/adv_20/market_cap — enrichment CLAUDE.md calls load-bearing for theme tickers. The fix needs a COALESCE-aware upsert or a separate on-demand write path. Small in shape, non-trivial in DB semantics. ▶ Also note the shipped export CTE has TWO halves with different lifespans: the stray-1-row-day half becomes dead the day the write site is fixed; the partial-failed-run half (a batch that dies at 40%) stays valuable FOREVER — no write-site fix prevents that. >> [ok:2026-08-13→2026-08-19 — not reached; the week went to The Real EP Plan, three live defects and #479. Re-dated with the reason, not rolled.] [ok:2026-08-18 CAPACITY TRIAGE, operator-approved — 15% of the weekly budget remains and 24 tasks were dated Wed-Fri, which was never going to happen. Split: the two approved evidence items + the deployed-but-unverified closes stay this week; **Lane C selection work moves to the WEEKEND when capacity fully resets** (better done properly Saturday than half-done Wednesday); the theme/rubric and data-hygiene block moves to next week as genuinely off the EP critical path. Operator: *"aligned. We can pull in any as appropirate later"* — so any of these can be pulled forward on demand; nothing here is blocked, only sequenced.]

Themes = no-money detection (ships full); re-granularization THRESHOLDS carry CHANGE_PROCESS N≥10._

Now both directions. **THE TWO ACCRUAL VEHICLES, which fire on their own and feed THIS task:** (1) `exit_tune_cohort_review` — recurring at n=20/40/60/80/100 closed live trades (today 14), carrying methods (a) per-trade forensic · (b) intraday partial sweep · (c0) regime segmentation · (c) character segmentation · (d) holding-period distribution, and now (e) stop geometry; (2) `exit_tune_bull_regime_read` — fires at 8 live closes in a BULL tape (today 0), owning the one cell nothing else can fill, since all 14 closed trades were non-bull. **THE DIVISION OF LABOUR:** the reviews are the SAMPLE CLOCK — they say WHEN there is enough data. #545 is the FRAME — it says WHICH variants are worth accruing toward, including the ones no trigger currently watches (next-day re-entry · N-day delayed setup · entry timing variants). Neither replaces the other, and both now say so in `data_gated_reviews.yaml` so a future run reports INTO this task rather than writing an orphan dated doc that #545 has to rediscover. ⚠ Deliberately NOT re-keyed onto a single trigger: a program that only wakes at n=20 cannot design its own grid, and the design work (the inventory + the parameter space) is answerable NOW from data already captured. >> 📌 **FIRST REAL INSTANCE OF THE DELAYED-ENTRY VARIANT — operator's own discretionary fill on TEAM, 2026-08-07, recorded while it is fresh and before the outcome is known.** He took the SAME stock Apollo was stopped out of, hours later, and is holding. **THE TWO ENTRIES SIDE BY SIDE, from prod + SIP bars:** Apollo — ORB entry $147.13 at 09:31, stop $143.21 (ORB low), **stopped out −$23.94** inside ~20 minutes. Operator — **$144.39 at ~11:50 ET, stop at the low of day.** ⚠ **The low of day was $141.51 at 13:55 ET — AFTER his entry** — so his stop moved with the session; it was the low-so-far at entry, not a fixed level. **His stated method (his words, and the imprecision is the point):** *"my entry is looking at the low forming, turn back up, I also look at some other technicals… no hard rule so hard to copy."* **WHAT THE TAPE SHOWS around that entry:** TEAM based $142.90-143.90 for ~45 min (15:30-16:00Z), then stair-stepped up — 16:05 c=143.91 · 16:10 c=144.29 · 16:15 c=144.67 · 16:20 c=145.16. He bought into the third rising bar of that turn, not the low itself. **Day's range: high $151.37 at the open, low $141.51 at 13:55, last $148.42 at 17:45Z** — i.e. the stock round-tripped and then recovered above his entry. ⚖ **WHY THIS MATTERS TO #545 AND NOT JUST AS AN ANECDOTE:** it is the concrete form of the *next-day / delayed re-entry* variant this program was filed to test, and the operator's framing is that the EP COHORT is fine while the TACTICS are not — this is him demonstrating the tactic manually on the exact name the system rejected. **The measurable question it poses:** for every Day-1 stop-out, does a re-entry keyed on *base-then-turn* (rather than on the ORB) beat doing nothing? That is answerable OFFLINE on the recorded stop-out set — no new capture needed, `mi_intraday_bars` + the minute-bar harnesses already hold it. ⚠ **The hard part, stated honestly: "looking at the low forming, turn back up" is not yet a rule.** Before it can be swept it needs a mechanical proxy (e.g. N consecutive higher closes / higher lows off a session low, with a time window), and the proxy is a modelling CHOICE that will not be faithful to what he actually does. Sweep several proxies rather than pick one and claim it represents him. ▶ OUTCOME OF THIS TRADE TO BE RECORDED WHEN IT CLOSES — one fill is not evidence, but it is the fixture. >> 📚 **620-CHART CAPTURED 2026-08-07 → `docs/methodology/620_chart.md`.** The operator named it as part of the TEAM entry above and asked whether we still had a record of it — **we did not, in memory or the repo; it had been shared in an earlier session and lost.** Now written down from the full source (Gil Morales, theowltrader.com 2023-05-25). **THE SPEC:** 5-min candles · 6-EMA (orange) + 20-EMA (blue) · MACD on the SAME 6/20 periods; the eSignal `(6,20,C,9)` trailing digit is an auto-fill the author explicitly does not tune. **THE RULE THAT MATTERS FOR A SWEEP: the MACD cross is the SIGNAL; the 6-over-20 EMA cross is CONFIRMATION and fires ~1 hour later, by which point price is 'well up and away from the original entry'.** Modelling the MA cross as the trigger would systematically enter late. Also: MACD **stretch** (fast pulling away from slow) = extension, and its FLATTENING is his take-profit; the 20-EMA is the guide rail (resistance on a bounce from below, entry trigger on a pullback from above). ⚠ **TWO THINGS THE SOURCE SAYS THAT CONSTRAIN HOW WE TEST IT.** (1) **Parameter sensitivity is measured by the author himself** — he compares (6,20) / (8,24) / (12,26) and reports *the lower the setting, the earlier the signal AND the greater the chance it is false.* So periods are an AXIS in the sweep, not a constant. (2) **It is subordinate to price.** In his NVDA example the short entry came from the $400 Century Mark on price alone and the MACD cross was only confirmation — *"it was not necessary to use the MACD and/or moving averages to generate short-sale entries"*. He calls it *"a tool, NOT a trading system"* twice. ⇒ **A 620-only proxy will underperform what the operator actually does, and that is expected, not a bug in the test.** It is the one SPECIFIABLE piece of his 'other technicals' — computable from `mi_intraday_bars` (1-min from 07-25, resampleable to 5-min) — so sweep it as one factor among several rather than as the rule. >> ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-07 — 'ZERO bull-tape trades' IS NO LONGER TRUE, and the nightly digest caught it before I did.** The regime axis on this line (and on `exit_tune_bull_regime_read`) was measured 08-06 as Correcting 7 / Choppy 6 / Crisis 1, **zero Bull**. I repeated that on 08-07 without re-running it. **Today's three entries — NET, FIGS, TEAM — were all taken in a Bull tape and all three stopped out (−1.0R combined).** So the live cohort is now 17 trades: Correcting 7 · Choppy 7 · Bull 3 · Crisis 1, and `exit_tune_bull_regime_read` stands at **3 of 8, not 0**. ⚠ The structural point SURVIVES — 14 of 17 are still non-bull, so conclusions drawn from the July cohort remain non-bull conclusions — but the ABSOLUTE claim does not, and any sweep must stop asserting the cell is empty. **The early bull read is not encouraging: 3 entries, 3 stops, same day-one pattern.** ⚠ PROCESS NOTE worth more than the number: the `sell_discipline` digest printed the correct split (`regime@entry: Correcting 7 · Choppy 4 · Bull 3`) on the same evening I was telling the operator it was zero. **A figure measured yesterday and quoted today is not a measurement.** Re-run regime/cohort counts at the moment of use — they move every trading day. [[verify-before-reporting-to-the-operator]] >> 💡 **OPERATOR VARIANT TO SWEEP, 2026-08-08 — a SECOND partial higher up:** *"another variation we can take (once we have runners) is if it makes sense to take another 1/3rd profit somewhere higher, like 10R or etc. to make sure we don't round trip a big winner."* ⚖ This is the right shape for his stated goal — the first partial stops the papercuts, a second one banks the outsized move before it round trips, and neither caps the runner the way an exit-all would. **Sweep it as an AXIS (second-partial level: 5R/8R/10R/15R × fraction), not a single candidate.** ⚠ He gated it himself — *"once we have runners"* — and the live cohort has never had one (17 trades, 0 winners, max hold 2 days), so this is **paper/replay-only until the live book produces a survivor.** Feeds the same replay harness as #548's work (`_508_exit_rule_replay.py` already models multi-leg partials). >> 📊 **5m RE-ENTRY REVIEW RUN 2026-08-09 (ripe 39d, the oldest on the board) — N DOES NOT SUPPORT A CONCLUSION, and one trade carries the whole result.** Ran over the FULL live stop-out set (17, not a sample). **Fired on 9 of 17 (53%)**; all 8 non-fires are genuine (price never returned to clear the post-stop 5-min range), none mechanical. Of the 9: **1 real hold (THC +12.43R — but on a 0.67% stop, a razor-thin R-unit), 2 partial-then-scratch, and 6 paid a SECOND FULL STOP** (CRCL/FIGS/HUT/QBTS/WKC/WULF, verified against official daily lows). **Net +7.76R — but excluding THC the other 8 net -4.67R.** Same failure mode the 1m version (0-for-7) was killed for; the one real difference is selectivity (53% vs firing on nearly every stop-out). ⚠ It fired on 2 of the 4 biggest forward movers and **paid a second stop on both** (HUT despite a +8.32R forward peak, QBTS despite +8.88R). ⚠ The review's own SQL has no account-mode filter and counted 38 (26 paper + 12 live) — which is how it registered ripe; the replayable live set is 17. - #313 | 2026-08-23 | pending | P6 Institution-grade ops (H1→H2): #256 split (done) + replay-everything CI (#302) + cost governance (per-role LLM spend vs P&L) [b2] [ok:#419 Phase-2 milestone re-date, operator-signed 7/5] >> ✅ **COST-GOVERNANCE INCREMENT SHIPPED 2026-08-02 — spend is now ATTRIBUTABLE PER LANE, and a lane that switches on ANNOUNCES ITSELF.** Operator, after the chart-vision shadow ran unnoticed: *"we can have hidden costs that provides no value that is running and no way for us to know about in time."* ⚠ **THE COST STACK WAS NOT THE FAILURE** — the #377 meter, the #378 board/alarm and the #379 per-caller anomaly + reduction detectors all worked; they were reading a bucket with TWO THINGS IN IT. `grade_holistic`'s `log_caller` had a **DEFAULT** of `ep_grade_judge`; the chart shadow took it, and **336 of the 379 calls in that bucket over 30 days ($12.90) were an experiment indistinguishable from live grading.** The dollars were always derivable — the ATTRIBUTION was not. `judge_divergence` had overridden the default correctly and the docstring explained why, so convention existed, was documented, and the next lane still didn't follow it. **(1) The default is GONE** — `log_caller` is REQUIRED; every lane names itself. The sweep found **8 unlabelled call sites, not 1** — every offline eval and replay was ALSO billing the live judge (`tape_judge_eval`, `judge_model_eval`, `judge_enrich_eval`, `judge_backfill_replay`, `selection_replay_268`, `judge_integration_probe`, `chart_axis_shadow`, `chart_axis_eval` are now distinct buckets). **(2) NEW-LANE DETECTOR** (`cost_board._new_lanes_from_rows` + the 17:52 ET watchdog): a caller with spend in the last 3d and ZERO across the trailing 30d Telegrams ONCE, ever (audit-log dedupe, the same idiom the 7/17 budget-alarm re-fire fix used). This closes a blind spot the code NAMED — `compute_caller_cost_anomalies` is cold-start gated *"so a brand-new caller can't false-positive on day 2"*, and the reduction heuristics only ask whether EXISTING spend is reducible, never whether a lane should exist. 28 tests. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a lane/purpose registry mapping each caller to what it buys** — it needs a "who consumes this" field nothing can verify, and #517 (filed TODAY) is live evidence that a registry without a forcing function becomes a pile. The weakest honest claim (*this started spending*) beats a richer one that rots. ✅ **VERIFIED-LIVE 2026-08-02 (deploy `0903575`, market-agent + execution).** Ran `compute_cost_watchdog` against REAL `api_usage` inside `apollo-market` (read-only — only the 17:52 ET job writes): clean run, 0 anomalies / 3 opportunities / **0 new lanes**, and CROSS-CHECKED against the raw table independently of the detector — the 5 most-recently-first-seen callers are all 6-17 days old, so zero is the CORRECT answer, not a silent failure. `judge_divergence` (first seen 7/27) is the worked example: it would have been announced on day one had this existed. ▶ **DEFERRED TO A WEEKDAY (flagged by the 8/02 /simplify altitude pass, NOT applied — money path, Sunday night, live judge grades at 09:45 ET):** the fix landed one layer too HIGH. `grade_holistic` now requires `log_caller`, but the true chokepoint is `judge_transport.invoke_forced_tool` — the function that actually calls `spend_tracker.log_anthropic_call_safe` — which still has `log_caller: Optional[str] = None`. **The residual hazard is WORSE than the one fixed**: a future forced-tool judge built directly on the transport (not via `grade_holistic`) would log NOTHING — no bucket at all, rather than the wrong bucket — and the AST test can't see it, because it hardcodes the three judge entry-point names. Verified: BOTH production call sites (`ep_grade_judge`, `mgmt_judge`) already pass a literal, so requiring it is zero-behaviour-change. ⚠ It is NOT free: `None = no logging` is a documented signed contract (#377, 'byte-identical to the pre-#377 path') with a test pinning it (`test_judge_logs_nothing_when_log_caller_none`), so this is a CONTRACT change, not a cleanup — it needs the deliberate deploy window, not a late-Sunday push. ⏭ ALSO CONSIDERED AND SKIPPED: moving the dedupe query into `db.py` per the CLAUDE.md single-SoT-for-queries rule. Two reviewers independently said leave it — `cost_board.py` already holds 3 inline cost-specific queries, and moving only the newest would split cost queries across two files, which is worse than the inconsistency it fixes. ▶ REMAINDER of #313 unchanged: replay-everything CI (#302) + spend-vs-P&L attribution. The Stage-2 BLOCKING fork is NOT taken — deliberately, on evidence.** The shadow had recorded **ZERO** `exposure_family_breach` events since it shipped 7/12, and that zero is HONEST, not broken instrumentation — I checked the acting population per [[shadow-zero-effect-check-instrumentation]]. **The live book has never been full:** 2026-05-01→07-27, on days holding anything — max concurrent **4** (once), **average 1.50**, only **4 days** with 2+, **1 day** with 3+. At threshold 2 an emit needs two EXISTING same-family positions plus the candidate = three of a book that has exceeded two positions twice ever. **So the premortem R1 risk ('5 same-family EP HIGHs fill all 5 slots = one bet at ~100% deployment') is real in principle and UNOBSERVED in practice — the binding constraint today is book SIZE, not correlation.** Shipping a blocking cap now would guard a zero-instance risk while adding a new failure mode to the entry path; that fork was explicitly declined. At threshold 1 an emit means 'this entry makes TWO positions share a theme' — the base rate that must exist before a cap can be argued for at all.
